gaokao_000_000 ✓ B B 8241 56.9 144.9 http://192.168.0.41:9292
Prompt
请解答以下数学题（单选题）。逐步推理，将最终答案放在 \boxed{} 中。

1. 样本数据6,8,4,5,12的中位数为（ ）
A. 5
B. 6
C. 8
D. 9

请将答案放在 \boxed{} 中。
Response
**逐步推理过程如下：**

1.  **理解中位数的定义**：
    中位数（Median）是指将一组数据按从小到大（或从大到小）的顺序排列后，位于中间位置的数值。
    *   如果数据的个数 $n$ 是奇数，中位数是第 $\frac{n+1}{2}$ 个数。
    *   如果数据的个数 $n$ 是偶数，中位数是中间两个数的平均值。

2.  **对数据进行排序**：
    给定的样本数据为：$6, 8, 4, 5, 12$。
    我们需要将这些数字按从小到大的顺序排列：
    *   最小的是 4
    *   其次是 5
    *   接着是 6
    *   然后是 8
    *   最大的是 12
    
    排序后的数据为：**4, 5, 6, 8, 12**。

3.  **确定中位数的位置**：
    这组数据共有 5 个数值（$n=5$）。
    因为 5 是奇数，所以中位数位于正中间的位置。
    位置计算：$\frac{5+1}{2} = 3$。
    即中位数是排序后第 3 个数。

4.  **找出中位数的值**：
    在排序后的序列 $4, 5, \mathbf{6}, 8, 12$ 中，第 3 个数是 **6**。

5.  **对照选项**：
    *   A. 5
    *   B. 6
    *   C. 8
    *   D. 9
    
    计算出的中位数 6 与选项 B 相符。

**结论**：
该样本数据的中位数为 6，对应选项 B。

\boxed{B}
Reasoning
Here's a thinking process that leads to the solution:

1.  **Analyze the Request:**
    *   **Question:** Find the median of the sample data set: 6, 8, 4, 5, 12.
    *   **Type:** Single-choice question.
    *   **Options:** A. 5, B. 6, C. 8, D. 9.
    *   **Requirement:** Step-by-step reasoning, final answer in `\boxed{}`.

2.  **Understand the Concept of Median:**
    *   The *median* of a data set is the middle value when the numbers are arranged in ascending (or descending) order.
    *   If the number of data points ($n$) is odd, the median is the value at position $(n+1)/2$.
    *   If the number of data points ($n$) is even, the median is the average of the two middle values (at positions $n/2$ and $n/2 + 1$).

3.  **Process the Given Data:**
    *   Data set: $\{6, 8, 4, 5, 12\}$.
    *   Count ($n$): There are 5 numbers. Since 5 is an odd number, there will be exactly one middle number.

4.  **Sort the Data:**
    *   Original order: 6, 8, 4, 5, 12.
    *   Arranging in ascending order:
        *   Smallest: 4
        *   Next: 5
        *   Next: 6
        *   Next: 8
        *   Largest: 12
    *   Sorted list: $4, 5, 6, 8, 12$.

5.  **Locate the Median:**
    *   $n = 5$.
    *   Position of median = $(5 + 1) / 2 = 3$.
    *   The 3rd number in the sorted list is 6.
    *   List indices:
        1.  4
        2.  5
        3.  **6**  <-- Median
        4.  8
        5.  12

6.  **Match with Options:**
    *   The calculated median is 6.
    *   Option A: 5
    *   Option B: 6
    *   Option C: 8
    *   Option D: 9
    *   The correct option is B.

7.  **Format the Output:**
    *   Write down the steps clearly.
    *   State the final answer inside `\boxed{}`.

8.  **Final Review:**
    *   Did I sort correctly? Yes (4 < 5 < 6 < 8 < 12).

    *   Does it match an option? Yes (Option B).

    *   Step 3: Select option.

2. 已知平面向量\vec{a}，\vec{b}不共线，且2\vec{a} + y\vec{b} = x\vec{a} - 3\vec{b}，则（ ）
A. x=2, y=-3
B. x=-2, y=3
C. x=2, y=3
D. x=-2, y=-3

请将答案放在 \boxed{} 中。
Response
**逐步推理：**

1. **分析已知条件**：题目给出平面向量 $\vec{a}$ 与 $\vec{b}$ **不共线**。根据平面向量基本定理，不共线的两个向量可以作为平面的一组基底，且平面内任意向量用这组基底表示时，表示形式是**唯一**的（即系数唯一）。

2. **整理等式**：将原方程 $2\vec{a} + y\vec{b} = x\vec{a} - 3\vec{b}$ 的同类项移到等号同一侧，得：
   $$(2 - x)\vec{a} + (y + 3)\vec{b} = \vec{0}$$

3. **利用基底性质列方程**：因为 $\vec{a}$ 与 $\vec{b}$ 不共线（线性无关），要使它们的线性组合等于零向量 $\vec{0}$，则各项系数必须同时为 $0$。因此可得方程组：
   $$
   \begin{cases}
   2 - x = 0 \\
   y + 3 = 0
   \end{cases}
   $$

4. **求解方程组**：
   - 由 $2 - x = 0$ 解得 $x = 2$
   - 由 $y + 3 = 0$ 解得 $y = -3$

5. **对照选项**：计算结果 $x=2,\ y=-3$ 与选项 A 完全一致。

**最终答案：**
\boxed{A}

   - $-\frac{1}{2}$ 在 $A$ 和 $B$ 中均存在

gaokao_000_003 ✓ D D 1371 67.7 20.3 http://192.168.0.41:9292
Prompt
请解答以下数学题（单选题）。逐步推理，将最终答案放在 \boxed{} 中。

4. 曲线y=5x+8lnx在点(1,5)处的切线方程为（ ）
A. y=3x+2
B. y=5x
C. y=8x-3
D. y=13x-8

请将答案放在 \boxed{} 中。
Response
我们需要求曲线 $y=5x+8\ln x$ 在点 $(1,5)$ 处的切线方程。

**第一步：求函数的导数**
切线的斜率等于函数在该点处的导数值。
对函数 $y=5x+8\ln x$ 求导：
$$y' = (5x)' + (8\ln x)' = 5 + 8 \cdot \frac{1}{x} = 5 + \frac{8}{x}$$

**第二步：计算切点处的斜率**
将切点的横坐标 $x=1$ 代入导数公式中：
$$k = y'|_{x=1} = 5 + \frac{8}{1} = 13$$
所以，切线的斜率为 $13$。

**第三步：写出切线方程**
已知切点为 $(1,5)$，斜率为 $13$。利用点斜式方程 $y - y_0 = k(x - x_0)$：
$$y - 5 = 13(x - 1)$$

**第四步：化简方程**
$$y - 5 = 13x - 13$$
$$y = 13x - 13 + 5$$
$$y = 13x - 8$$

对比选项：
A. $y=3x+2$
B. $y=5x$
C. $y=8x-3$
D. $y=13x-8$

计算结果与选项 D 一致。

\boxed{D}
Reasoning
Here's a thinking process, simulating the step-by-step reasoning required to solve the math problem:

1.  **Understand the Goal:** The user wants to find the equation of the tangent line to the curve $y = 5x + 8\ln x$ at the point $(1, 5)$. The options are A, B, C, D. I need to provide the reasoning and the final answer in a specific format `\boxed{}`.

2.  **Identify the Given Information:**
    *   Curve equation: $y = 5x + 8\ln x$
    *   Point of tangency: $(x_0, y_0) = (1, 5)$
    *   Goal: Find the tangent line equation.

3.  **Recall the Steps to Find a Tangent Line:**
    *   Step 1: Verify the point lies on the curve (optional but good practice).
    *   Step 2: Find the derivative of the function, $y'$, which represents the slope of the tangent line at any point $x$.
    *   Step 3: Evaluate the derivative at the given $x$-coordinate ($x_0 = 1$) to find the specific slope $k$.
    *   Step 4: Use the point-slope form of the line equation: $y - y_0 = k(x - x_0)$.
    *   Step 5: Simplify the equation to slope-intercept form ($y = mx + b$) to match the options.

4.  **Execute Step 1: Verify the Point.**
    *   $x = 1$
    *   $y = 5(1) + 8\ln(1)$
    *   Since $\ln(1) = 0$, $y = 5 + 0 = 5$.
    *   The point $(1, 5)$ is indeed on the curve.

5.  **Execute Step 2: Find the Derivative.**
    *   Function: $y = 5x + 8\ln x$
    *   Differentiate with respect to $x$:
        *   Derivative of $5x$ is $5$.
        *   Derivative of $8\ln x$ is $8 \cdot \frac{1}{x} = \frac{8}{x}$.
    *   So, $y' = 5 + \frac{8}{x}$.

6.  **Execute Step 3: Calculate the Slope at $x = 1$.**
    *   $k = y'(1) = 5 + \frac{8}{1}$
    *   $k = 5 + 8 = 13$.
    *   The slope of the tangent line is $13$.

7.  **Execute Step 4: Formulate the Equation.**
    *   Point: $(1, 5)$
    *   Slope: $13$
    *   Point-slope form: $y - 5 = 13(x - 1)$

8.  **Execute Step 5: Simplify.**
    *   $y - 5 = 13x - 13$
    *   $y = 13x - 13 + 5$
    *   $y = 13x - 8$

9.  **Compare with Options:**
    *   A. $y = 3x + 2$
    *   B. $y = 5x$
    *   C. $y = 8x - 3$
    *   D. $y = 13x - 8$

    My calculated equation $y = 13x - 8$ matches Option D.

**逐步推理：**

1. **求 $C_1$ 的焦点坐标**
   抛物线 $C_1$ 的方程为 $y^2 = 2p_1 x$。已知其经过点 $(4,8)$，代入方程得：
   $$8^2 = 2p_1 \times 4 \implies 64 = 8p_1 \implies p_1 = 8$$
   对于开口向右的标准抛物线 $y^2 = 2px$，其焦点坐标为 $\left(\frac{p}{2}, 0\right)$。
   因此，$C_1$ 的焦点为 $F_1\left(\frac{8}{2}, 0\right) = (4, 0)$。

2. **求 $C_2$ 的焦点坐标**
   抛物线 $C_2$ 的方程为 $x^2 = 2p_2 y$。已知其经过点 $(4,8)$，代入方程得：
   $$4^2 = 2p_2 \times 8 \implies 16 = 16p_2 \implies p_2 = 1$$
   对于开口向上的标准抛物线 $x^2 = 2py$，其焦点坐标为 $\left(0, \frac{p}{2}\right)$。
   因此，$C_2$ 的焦点为 $F_2\left(0, \frac{1}{2}\right)$。

3. **计算两焦点之间的距离**
   利用两点间距离公式，计算 $F_1(4, 0)$ 与 $F_2(0, \frac{1}{2})$ 的距离：
   $$|F_1F_2| = \sqrt{(4 - 0)^2 + \left(0 - \frac{1}{2}\right)^2} = \sqrt{16 + \frac{1}{4}} = \sqrt{\frac{65}{4}} = \frac{\sqrt{65}}{2}$$

对比题目选项，计算结果与选项 D 完全一致。

gaokao_000_005 ✓ B B 8045 64.3 125.2 http://192.168.0.41:9292
Prompt
请解答以下数学题（单选题）。逐步推理，将最终答案放在 \boxed{} 中。

6. 已知函数f(x)=\frac{x+2}{e^x+a}的最大值为1，则a = （ ）
A. \frac{1}{2}
B. 1
C. \frac{3}{2}
D. 2

请将答案放在 \boxed{} 中。
Response
要确定常数 $a$ 使得函数 $f(x)=\frac{x+2}{e^x+a}$ 的最大值为 1，我们可以通过以下步骤进行推理：

### 1. 求导并分析极值点
首先求 $f(x)$ 的导数 $f'(x)$。利用商的求导法则：
$$f'(x) = \frac{(x+2)'(e^x+a) - (x+2)(e^x+a)'}{(e^x+a)^2} = \frac{1 \cdot (e^x+a) - (x+2)e^x}{(e^x+a)^2}$$
化简分子得：
$$f'(x) = \frac{e^x + a - xe^x - 2e^x}{(e^x+a)^2} = \frac{a - e^x(1+x)}{(e^x+a)^2}$$

当函数取得最大值时，$f'(x) = 0$。令分子为零，得到极值点 $x_0$ 满足的条件：
$$a = e^{x_0}(1+x_0) \quad \dots \text{(1)}$$

### 2. 利用最大值条件求解
题目已知 $f(x)$ 的最大值为 1，即 $f(x_0) = 1$：
$$\frac{x_0+2}{e^{x_0}+a} = 1 \implies x_0+2 = e^{x_0}+a \quad \dots \text{(2)}$$

将方程 (1) 中的 $a$ 代入方程 (2)：
$$x_0+2 = e^{x_0} + e^{x_0}(1+x_0)$$
$$x_0+2 = e^{x_0}(1 + 1 + x_0)$$
$$x_0+2 = e^{x_0}(x_0+2)$$

移项并提取公因式 $(x_0+2)$：
$$(x_0+2)(1 - e^{x_0}) = 0$$

这给出了两个可能的解：
1.  $x_0+2 = 0 \implies x_0 = -2$
2.  $1 - e^{x_0} = 0 \implies x_0 = 0$

### 3. 验证与确定 $a$ 的值
*   **若 $x_0 = -2$**：此时 $f(x_0) = \frac{-2+2}{e^{-2}+a} = 0$，与题目已知最大值 1 不符，故舍去。
*   **若 $x_0 = 0$**：将 $x_0 = 0$ 代入条件 (1)：
    $$a = e^0(1+0) = 1 \cdot 1 = 1$$

验证：当 $a=1$ 时，$f(x) = \frac{x+2}{e^x+1}$，$f(0) = \frac{2}{1+1} = 1$，符合题意。

### 结论
常数 $a$ 的值为 1。

最终答案是 \boxed{B}。
Reasoning
Here's a thinking process to solve the problem:

1.  **Understand the Goal:** The objective is to find the value of the constant $a$ such that the maximum value of the function $f(x) = \frac{x+2}{e^x + a}$ is equal to 1.

2.  **Analyze the Function:**
    *   $f(x) = \frac{x+2}{e^x + a}$
    *   Domain: $e^x + a \neq 0$. Since $e^x > 0$, if $a \ge 0$, the denominator is never zero. Looking at the options, $a$ is positive, so the domain is $\mathbb{R}$.
    *   We need to find the maximum value. This usually involves finding critical points where the derivative is zero.

3.  **Compute the Derivative:**
    Let $u = x+2$ and $v = e^x + a$. Then $f(x) = \frac{u}{v}$.
    $f'(x) = \frac{u'v - uv'}{v^2}$
    $u' = 1$
    $v' = e^x$
    $f'(x) = \frac{1 \cdot (e^x + a) - (x+2) \cdot e^x}{(e^x + a)^2}$
    $f'(x) = \frac{e^x + a - xe^x - 2e^x}{(e^x + a)^2}$
    $f'(x) = \frac{a - e^x - xe^x}{(e^x + a)^2}$
    $f'(x) = \frac{a - e^x(1+x)}{(e^x + a)^2}$

4.  **Find Critical Points:**
    Set $f'(x) = 0$.
    The denominator is always positive (assuming $a>0$). So, we need the numerator to be zero.
    $a - e^x(1+x) = 0$
    $a = e^x(1+x)$

    Let $g(x) = e^x(1+x)$. We need to find $x$ such that $g(x) = a$.
    Let's analyze $g(x)$.
    $g'(x) = e^x(1+x) + e^x(1) = e^x(2+x)$.
    $g'(x) = 0$ when $x = -2$.
    For $x < -2$, $g'(x) < 0$ (decreasing).
    For $x > -2$, $g'(x) > 0$ (increasing).
    Minimum value of $g(x)$ is at $x = -2$, which is $g(-2) = e^{-2}(1-2) = -e^{-2}$.
    Since $a$ is a positive constant (from options), there will be solutions for $x$. Since $g(x)$ goes from $-\infty$ to $-\frac{1}{e^2}$ to $\infty$, for any $a > 0$, there are two intersection points or one? Actually, since $a>0$ and the minimum is negative, there is exactly one intersection point for $x > -2$ (since $g(-1)=0$) and potentially one for $x < -2$? No, wait.
    Let's check the range.
    $x \to -\infty$, $g(x) \to 0$ (from positive side? No. $e^x \to 0$, $1+x \to -\infty$. Limit is 0).
    Let's look at the shape.
    At $x = -2$, $g(x) < 0$.
    At $x = -1$, $g(x) = 0$.
    For $x > -1$, $g(x) > 0$.
    For $x < -1$, $g(x) < 0$ (mostly, except near $-\infty$ where it approaches 0 from below).
    Actually, $e^x > 0$. Sign of $g(x)$ is sign of $(1+x)$.
    So $g(x) > 0$ when $x > -1$.
    $g(x) < 0$ when $x < -1$.
    Since $a > 0$ (options are positive), the equation $a = e^x(1+x)$ implies we must have $x > -1$.
    In the interval $x > -1$, $g(x)$ is strictly increasing because $g'(x) = e^x(x+2)$ and $x+2 > 1 > 0$.
    So there is a unique solution $x_0$ for $f'(x)=0$ where $x_0 > -1$.
    Let's check the sign change of $f'(x)$ around $x_0$.
    Numerator is $a - g(x)$.
    For $x < x_0$ (but $x > -1$), $g(x) < a$, so numerator $> 0$, $f'(x) > 0$.
    For $x > x_0$, $g(x) > a$, so numerator $< 0$, $f'(x) < 0$.
    Thus, $x_0$ is a local maximum.
    What about $x < -1$? Here $g(x) < 0 < a$, so numerator is always positive. $f'(x) > 0$.
    So the function increases for all $x < x_0$ and decreases for $x > x_0$.
    Therefore, $x_0$ is the global maximum point.

5.  **Apply the Condition:**
    The maximum value is 1.
    So, $f(x_0) = 1$.
    $x_0$ satisfies two conditions:
    1.  $a = e^{x_0}(1+x_0)$  (from $f'(x_0)=0$)
    2.  $\frac{x_0+2}{e^{x_0}+a} = 1$ (from max value = 1)

6.  **Solve the System of Equations:**
    From (2):
    $x_0 + 2 = e^{x_0} + a$
    $\Rightarrow a = x_0 + 2 - e^{x_0}$

    Substitute this expression for $a$ into (1):
    $x_0 + 2 - e^{x_0} = e^{x_0}(1+x_0)$
    $x_0 + 2 - e^{x_0} = e^{x_0} + x_0 e^{x_0}$
    Rearrange terms to group $x_0$:
    $x_0 + 2 = e^{x_0} + e^{x_0} + x_0 e^{x_0}$
    $x_0 + 2 = 2e^{x_0} + x_0 e^{x_0}$
    $x_0 + 2 = e^{x_0}(2 + x_0)$

    Let's look at this equation:
    $(x_0 + 2) = e^{x_0}(x_0 + 2)$
    $(x_0 + 2) - e^{x_0}(x_0 + 2) = 0$
    $(x_0 + 2)(1 - e^{x_0}) = 0$

    This product is zero if either factor is zero.
    Case 1: $x_0 + 2 = 0 \Rightarrow x_0 = -2$.
    Case 2: $1 - e^{x_0} = 0 \Rightarrow e^{x_0} = 1 \Rightarrow x_0 = 0$.

    Let's check validity of each case.

    **Case 1: $x_0 = -2$**
    Check the derivative condition $a = e^{x_0}(1+x_0)$.
    $a = e^{-2}(1 + (-2)) = e^{-2}(-1) = -e^{-2}$.
    But the options for $a$ are positive numbers ($1/2, 1, 3/2, 2$). Also, usually parameters in such contexts are positive, but strictly speaking, the problem doesn't state $a>0$. However, if $a = -e^{-2}$, the denominator $e^x + a$ would be zero at $x=-2$, making the function undefined at the maximum point. That doesn't make sense.
    Let's check the function value if $x_0 = -2$. $f(-2) = 0 / (e^{-2} + a) = 0 \neq 1$.
    So $x_0 = -2$ is not the solution.

    **Case 2: $x_0 = 0$**
    Check the derivative condition $a = e^{x_0}(1+x_0)$.
    $x_0 = 0 \Rightarrow a = e^0(1+0) = 1 \cdot 1 = 1$.
    So $a = 1$.
    Now check if this is consistent with the max value condition.
    If $a = 1$, then $f(x) = \frac{x+2}{e^x + 1}$.
    We found the critical point at $x_0 = 0$.
    Calculate $f(0)$:
    $f(0) = \frac{0+2}{e^0 + 1} = \frac{2}{1+1} = \frac{2}{2} = 1$.
    This matches the given condition that the maximum value is 1.

7.  **Verify the Solution:**
    If $a=1$, $f(x) = \frac{x+2}{e^x+1}$.
    Derivative $f'(x) = \frac{1 - e^x(1+x)}{(e^x+1)^2}$.
    Critical point when $e^x(1+x) = 1$.
    Let $h(x) = e^x(1+x) - 1$.
    $h(0) = 1(1) - 1 = 0$. So $x=0$ is a root.
    $h'(x) = e^x(1+x) + e^x = e^x(2+x)$.
    For $x > -2$, $h'(x) > 0$, so $h(x)$ is increasing.
    Since $h(0)=0$, for $x<0$, $h(x)<0 \Rightarrow f'(x)>0$.
    For $x>0$, $h(x)>0 \Rightarrow f'(x)<0$.
    So $x=0$ is indeed the global maximum.
    Max value is $f(0) = 1$.
    Everything holds.
    So $a=1$.

8.  **Match with Options:**
    A. 1/2
    B. 1
    C. 3/2
    D. 2

    The calculated value $a=1$ corresponds to option B.

9.  **Double Check Steps:**
    *   Derivative calculation:
        $f(x) = (x+2)(e^x+a)^{-1}$
        $f'(x) = 1 \cdot (e^x+a)^{-1} + (x+2) \cdot (-1)(e^x+a)^{-2} \cdot e^x$
        $f'(x) = \frac{1}{e^x+a} - \frac{e^x(x+2)}{(e^x+a)^2}$
        $f'(x) = \frac{e^x+a - xe^x - 2e^x}{(e^x+a)^2} = \frac{a - e^x(1+x)}{(e^x+a)^2}$. Correct.
    *   Critical point condition: $a = e^x(1+x)$. Correct.
    *   Max value condition: $f(x) = 1 \Rightarrow x+2 = e^x+a$. Correct.
    *   Substitution: $a = x+2-e^x$.
        $x+2-e^x = e^x(1+x) = e^x + xe^x$.
        $x+2 = 2e^x + xe^x = e^x(2+x)$.
        $(x+2)(1-e^x) = 0$. Correct.
    *   Solutions for $x$: $x=-2$ or $x=0$.
    *   Analysis of $x=-2$: $a = e^{-2}(-1) < 0$. Denominator at $x=-2$ is $e^{-2} + a = 0$. Undefined. Also $f(-2)=0 \neq 1$. Discard.
    *   Analysis of $x=0$: $a = e^0(1) = 1$. Denominator $e^0+1 = 2 \neq 0$. $f(0) = 2/2 = 1$. Valid.
    *   Result $a=1$.

10. **Alternative Approach (Tangent Method):**
    The maximum value is 1. This means the curve $y = f(x)$ touches the line $y=1$ at the peak.
    So, there exists an $x_0$ such that:
    1.  $f(x_0) = 1$
    2.  $f'(x_0) = 0$

    From 1: $\frac{x_0+2}{e^{x_0}+a} = 1 \implies x_0+2 = e^{x_0}+a \implies a = x_0+2-e^{x_0}$.
    From 2: $f'(x_0) = 0 \implies a - e^{x_0}(1+x_0) = 0 \implies a = e^{x_0}(1+x_0)$.

    Equating expressions for $a$:
    $x_0+2-e^{x_0} = e^{x_0}(1+x_0)$
    $x_0+2 = 2e^{x_0} + x_0 e^{x_0}$
    $x_0+2 = e^{x_0}(2+x_0)$
    $(x_0+2)(1-e^{x_0}) = 0$
    Same result.

    Is there any constraint on $a$? The problem implies $a$ is a constant. The options are positive.
    Is it possible that the maximum is not at a critical point?
    For rational functions involving exponentials, maxima are usually at critical points.
    Behavior at infinity:
    As $x \to \infty$, $f(x) \approx \frac{x}{e^x} \to 0$.
    As $x \to -\infty$, $e^x \to 0$, so $f(x) \approx \frac{x+2}{a} \to -\infty$ (assuming $a>0$).
    So the global maximum must occur at a critical point where $f'(x)=0$.

    Wait, check $x \to -\infty$.
    $f(x) = \frac{x+2}{e^x+a}$.
    If $a>0$, denominator $\to a$. Numerator $\to -\infty$. So $f(x) \to -\infty$.
    If $a=0$ (not an option), denominator $\to 0^+$, numerator $\to -\infty$. $f(x) \to -\infty$?
    Actually if $a=0$, $f(x) = (x+2)e^{-x}$. As $x \to -\infty$, $e^{-x} \to \infty$, $x+2 \to -\infty$, product $\to -\infty$.
    So max is finite.

    Let's check the options just to be sure.
    If $a=1/2$:
    $a = 0.5$.
    Solve $0.5 = e^x(1+x)$.
    Try $x=0 \implies 1 \neq 0.5$.
    Try $x=-0.5 \implies e^{-0.5}(0.5) \approx 0.606 \cdot 0.5 \approx 0.3 < 0.5$.
    Solution exists. Let's call it $x_1$.
    Max value $f(x_1) = \frac{x_1+2}{e^{x_1}+0.5}$.
    From $a=e^{x_1}(1+x_1)$, $e^{x_1} = \frac{a}{1+x_1}$.
    $f(x_1) = \frac{x_1+2}{\frac{a}{1+x_1} + a} = \frac{x_1+2}{a(\frac{1}{1+x_1} + 1)} = \frac{x_1+2}{a(\frac{1+1+x_1}{1+x_1})} = \frac{(x_1+2)(1+x_1)}{a(x_1+2)} = \frac{1+x_1}{a}$.
    We want max value = 1.
    So $\frac{1+x_1}{a} = 1 \implies 1+x_1 = a \implies x_1 = a-1$.
    Substitute $x_1 = a-1$ back into critical point equation $a = e^{x_1}(1+x_1)$.
    $a = e^{a-1}(1 + a - 1) = e^{a-1} \cdot a$.
    $a = a e^{a-1}$.
    Since $a \neq 0$ (from options), divide by $a$:
    $1 = e^{a-1}$.
    Taking ln: $0 = a-1 \implies a = 1$.
    This confirms the previous result derived algebraically.
    This derivation $x_1 = a-1$ is much faster/cleaner.
    Let's re-verify this logic.

    **Logic Check:**
    1.  Critical point condition: $f'(x) = 0 \implies a = e^x(1+x)$.
    2.  At the maximum point $x$, the value is $f(x) = \frac{x+2}{e^x+a}$.
    3.  We are given max value = 1, so $\frac{x+2}{e^x+a} = 1 \implies x+2 = e^x+a$.
    4.  From (1), $e^x = \frac{a}{1+x}$. Note $x \neq -1$.
    5.  Substitute $e^x$ into (3): $x+2 = \frac{a}{1+x} + a$.
    6.  $x+2 = a(\frac{1}{1+x} + 1) = a(\frac{1 + 1 + x}{1+x}) = a(\frac{x+2}{1+x})$.
    7.  Since $f(x)=1$ and numerator is $x+2$, $x+2$ cannot be 0 (otherwise $f(x)=0$). So $x \neq -2$.
    8.  Divide by $x+2$: $1 = \frac{a}{1+x}$.
    9.  $1+x = a \implies x = a-1$.
    10. Now substitute $x = a-1$ into the critical point equation $a = e^x(1+x)$.
    11. $a = e^{a-1}(1 + (a-1)) = e^{a-1} \cdot a$.
    12. $a = a e^{a-1}$.
    13. Since $a$ is from options (non-zero), $1 = e^{a-1}$.
    14. $a-1 = 0 \implies a = 1$.

    This confirms $a=1$ is the unique solution for the max value being 1.

    Let's check if $a$ could be 0? No, options don't have 0. If $a=0$, $f(x) = (x+2)e^{-x}$. Max at $x=-1$?
    $f'(x) = e^{-x} - (x+2)e^{-x} = e^{-x}(1 - x - 2) = -e^{-x}(x+1)$.
    Critical point $x=-1$.
    $f(-1) = (-1+2)e^1 = e \approx 2.718 \neq 1$.
    So $a \neq 0$.

    So the derivation holds. $a=1$.

11. **Final Conclusion:**
    The value of $a$ is 1.
    This corresponds to Option B.

    Let's write down the steps clearly.

    Let $h(x) = 1 - e^x(1+x)$.
    $h'(x) = -[e^x(1+x) + e^x] = -e^x(x+2)$.
    At $x=0$, $h'(0) = -1(2) = -2 < 0$.

    For $x>0$ (small), $h(x) < 0 \implies f'(x) < 0$.
    So $x=0$ is a local maximum.
    Since it's the only critical point for $x>-1$ and function goes to 0 at $\infty$ and $-\infty$ at $-\infty$, it's the global max.

Prompt
请解答以下数学题（单选题）。逐步推理，将最终答案放在 \boxed{} 中。

7. 一百零八塔位于宁夏回族自治区青铜峡市，以其独特的建筑格局和深厚的历史文化闻名遐迩。该塔群共有108座塔，依山势自上而下排成12行，将第i行中塔的座数记为a_i(i=1,2,...,12)，其中a_1=1, a_2=a_3=3, a_4=a_5=5，且a_6, a_7, ..., a_{12}是一个首项为7，公差为2的等差数列。将a_1, a_2, ..., a_{12}分为6组，每组2个数，使得每组的2个数之和可构成一个项数为6且公差为d(d>0)的等差数列，则d = （ ）
A. 2
B. 4
C. 6
D. 8

请将答案放在 \boxed{} 中。
Response
### 逐步推理：

1. **列出所有 $a_i$ 的值**
   根据题意：
   - $a_1 = 1$
   - $a_2 = a_3 = 3$
   - $a_4 = a_5 = 5$
   - $a_6, a_7, \dots, a_{12}$ 是首项为 $7$、公差为 $2$ 的等差数列，依次为：$7, 9, 11, 13, 15, 17, 19$。
   
   将12个数汇总为集合：$\{1, 3, 3, 5, 5, 7, 9, 11, 13, 15, 17, 19\}$。
   验证总数：$1+3+3+5+5+7+9+11+13+15+17+19 = 108$，与题意相符。

2. **分析奇偶性**
   观察上述12个数，发现它们**全部是奇数**。
   将任意两个奇数相加，其和必为**偶数**。
   因此，分组后得到的6个两数之和 $S_1, S_2, S_3, S_4, S_5, S_6$ 必须全部为偶数。

3. **利用总和建立方程**
   这6个和构成公差为 $d$ ($d>0$) 的等差数列。根据等差数列求和公式，这6个和的总和等于所有塔数之和：
   $$S_1 + S_2 + S_3 + S_4 + S_5 + S_6 = 108$$
   等差数列前6项和公式为 $\frac{6}{2}[2S_1 + (6-1)d] = 3(2S_1 + 5d)$，故有：
   $$3(2S_1 + 5d) = 108 \implies 2S_1 + 5d = 36$$

4. **结合条件筛选选项**
   - 由第2步知，$S_1$ 必为偶数，设 $S_1 = 2k$ ($k \in \mathbb{Z}^+$)，则 $2S_1 = 4k$ 必为 $4$ 的倍数。
   - 代入方程：$4k + 5d = 36 \implies 5d = 36 - 4k$。
   - 因为 $36$ 和 $4k$ 都能被 $4$ 整除，所以 $5d$ 也必须能被 $4$ 整除。由于 $5$ 与 $4$ 互质，故 **$d$ 必须是 $4$ 的倍数**。
   - 查看选项：A. 2，B. 4，C. 6，D. 8。其中 $4$ 和 $8$ 是 $4$ 的倍数。
   - 再由 $S_1 > 0$ 可得 $2S_1 = 36 - 5d > 0 \implies 5d < 36 \implies d \le 7$。
   - 排除 $d=8$，唯一符合条件的选项为 $d=4$。

5. **验证可行性（可选）**
   当 $d=4$ 时，$2S_1 = 16 \Rightarrow S_1 = 8$。六个和依次为 $8, 12, 16, 20, 24, 28$。
   可成功配对验证：$(3,5)=8,\ (1,11)=12,\ (3,13)=16,\ (5,15)=20,\ (7,17)=24,\ (9,19)=28$。完全符合题意。

综上所述，公差 $d=4$，对应选项 B。

\boxed{B}
Reasoning
Here's a thinking process:

1.  **Understand the Problem:**
   - There are 108 towers arranged in 12 rows.
   - Let $a_i$ be the number of towers in the $i$-th row, for $i=1, 2, \dots, 12$.
   - Given: $a_1 = 1$, $a_2 = a_3 = 3$, $a_4 = a_5 = 5$.
   - For $i=6, 7, \dots, 12$, $a_i$ forms an arithmetic sequence with first term 7 and common difference 2. So $a_6 = 7, a_7 = 9, a_8 = 11, a_9 = 13, a_{10} = 15, a_{11} = 17, a_{12} = 19$.
   - Let's list all $a_i$:
     $a_1 = 1$
     $a_2 = 3, a_3 = 3$
     $a_4 = 5, a_5 = 5$
     $a_6 = 7, a_7 = 9, a_8 = 11, a_9 = 13, a_{10} = 15, a_{11} = 17, a_{12} = 19$
     Wait, let's verify the total number of towers: $1 + 3 + 3 + 5 + 5 + 7 + 9 + 11 + 13 + 15 + 17 + 19$.
     Sum = $1 + 6 + 10 + (7+19)\times 7 / 2 = 1 + 6 + 10 + 26 \times 7 / 2 = 17 + 91 = 108$. Matches the problem statement. Good.
   - The problem says: Divide the 12 numbers $a_1, \dots, a_{12}$ into 6 groups, each containing 2 numbers. Let the sums of the pairs be $s_1, s_2, \dots, s_6$. These sums form an arithmetic sequence with 6 terms and common difference $d > 0$. Find $d$.

2.  **Analyze the Sums:**
   - We have 12 numbers: $\{1, 3, 3, 5, 5, 7, 9, 11, 13, 15, 17, 19\}$.
   - We need to pair them up into 6 pairs such that their sums form an AP.
   - Let the sums be $S_1, S_2, S_3, S_4, S_5, S_6$. They form an AP with difference $d$.
   - The total sum of all $a_i$ is 108.
   - Since each pair sums to $S_k$, the sum of all $S_k$ is also 108.
   - So, $S_1 + S_2 + S_3 + S_4 + S_5 + S_6 = 108$.
   - For an AP with 6 terms, the sum is $\frac{6}{2}(S_1 + S_6) = 3(S_1 + S_6) = 108 \Rightarrow S_1 + S_6 = 36$.
   - Also, $S_6 = S_1 + 5d$. So $2S_1 + 5d = 36$.
   - Since $S_1$ and $S_6$ are sums of two integers from the set, they must be integers. Also $d$ must be an integer (or rational, but likely integer given options).
   - Possible values for $d$ from options: 2, 4, 6, 8.
   - Let's test each option.

3.  **Test Options:**
   - **Case d = 2:**
     $2S_1 + 5(2) = 36 \Rightarrow 2S_1 = 26 \Rightarrow S_1 = 13$.
     Sums would be: 13, 15, 17, 19, 21, 23.
     Can we pair the numbers to get these sums?
     Max possible sum: $19 + 17 = 36$. Min possible sum: $1 + 3 = 4$.
     The sums we need are up to 23. That's fine.
     Let's check if we can form these sums.
     We need a pair summing to 23. Available numbers: 19, 17, 15, 13, 11, 9, 7, 5, 5, 3, 3, 1.
     19 + ? = 23 $\Rightarrow$ 4. Not in set.
     17 + ? = 23 $\Rightarrow$ 6. Not in set.
     15 + ? = 23 $\Rightarrow$ 8. Not in set.
     13 + ? = 23 $\Rightarrow$ 10. Not in set.
     So $d=2$ is impossible.

   - **Case d = 4:**
     $2S_1 + 5(4) = 36 \Rightarrow 2S_1 = 16 \Rightarrow S_1 = 8$.
     Sums: 8, 12, 16, 20, 24, 28.
     Need a pair summing to 28. Max sum is $19+17=36$. $19+9=28$. Yes.
     Need a pair summing to 24. $17+7=24$ or $15+9=24$ or $13+11=24$.
     Need a pair summing to 20.
     Need a pair summing to 16.
     Need a pair summing to 12.
     Need a pair summing to 8.
     Let's try to construct it.
     Set: $\{1, 3, 3, 5, 5, 7, 9, 11, 13, 15, 17, 19\}$
     Pair for 28: $(19, 9)$. Remaining: $\{1, 3, 3, 5, 5, 7, 11, 13, 15, 17\}$.
     Pair for 24: $(17, 7)$. Remaining: $\{1, 3, 3, 5, 5, 11, 13, 15\}$.
     Pair for 20: $(15, 5)$. Remaining: $\{1, 3, 3, 5, 11, 13\}$.
     Pair for 16: $(13, 3)$. Remaining: $\{1, 3, 5, 11\}$.
     Pair for 12: $(11, 1)$. Remaining: $\{3, 5\}$.
     Pair for 8: $(5, 3)$. Works!
     So $d=4$ is possible.

   - Let's quickly check $d=6$ and $d=8$ just to be sure.
   - **Case d = 6:**
     $2S_1 + 30 = 36 \Rightarrow S_1 = 3$.
     Sums: 3, 9, 15, 21, 27, 33.
     Max sum possible is $19+17=36$. 33 is possible ($19+14$ no, $17+16$ no, $15+18$ no). Wait, check combinations for 33:
     $19+13=32$, $19+15=34$. No pair sums to 33. All numbers are odd except... wait, all numbers in the set are odd!
     Let's check parity: $\{1, 3, 3, 5, 5, 7, 9, 11, 13, 15, 17, 19\}$. All are odd.
     Sum of two odd numbers is always even.
     So every pair sum must be even.
     For $d=6$, sums are 3, 9, 15, 21, 27, 33. All are odd. Impossible.
     For $d=2$, sums were 13, 15, 17, 19, 21, 23. All odd. Impossible.

     So only $d=4$ works, and it yields all even sums: 8, 12, 16, 20, 24, 28. All even, consistent with parity argument.

Prompt
请解答以下数学题（单选题）。逐步推理，将最终答案放在 \boxed{} 中。

8. 设U={(x_1, x_2, x_3)|x_i \in \{-2,-1,1,2"}, i=1,2,3}\}为空间中64个点构成的集合，点P(1,1,1)，记样本空间\Omega = C_U P，从\Omega中随机取一个点，定义随机变量X如下：对\Omega中的每个点A(x_1, x_2, x_3)，令X(A) = x_1 + x_2 + x_3，则X的数学期望值为（ ）
A. -\frac{1}{21}
B. -\frac{1}{63}
C. 0
D. \frac{1}{7}

请将答案放在 \boxed{} 中。
Response
这是一道关于概率统计的数学题，主要考察离散型随机变量的数学期望计算。以下是逐步推理过程：

### 1. 分析样本空间与总点数

*   **集合 $U$ 的定义**：集合 $U$ 由点 $(x_1, x_2, x_3)$ 组成，其中每个坐标 $x_i$ 只能取 $\{-2, -1, 1, 2\}$ 这 4 个值。
*   **$U$ 的元素个数**：由于有 3 个坐标，每个坐标有 4 种选择，因此集合 $U$ 的总点数为 $4 \times 4 \times 4 = 64$ 个。
*   **样本空间 $\Omega$**：题目定义 $\Omega = C_U P$，即 $\Omega$ 是 $U$ 中除去点 $P(1, 1, 1)$ 后的集合。
*   **$\Omega$ 的元素个数**：由于点 $P(1, 1, 1)$ 在集合 $U$ 中，因此 $\Omega$ 的点数为 $64 - 1 = 63$ 个。

### 2. 计算全集 $U$ 的坐标和

定义随机变量 $X(A) = x_1 + x_2 + x_3$。我们先计算全集 $U$ 中所有点的 $X$ 值之和 $S_U$。

*   **坐标 $x_1$ 的总和**：在 $U$ 的 64 个点中，每个 $x_1$ 的具体数值都会出现 $4 \times 4 = 16$ 次。因此，$x_1$ 的总和为：
    $$16 \times (-2 + (-1) + 1 + 2) = 16 \times 0 = 0$$
*   **坐标 $x_2$ 和 $x_3$ 的总和**：同理，由于对称性，$x_2$ 和 $x_3$ 的总和也均为 0。
*   **全集 $U$ 的总和**：$S_U = 0 + 0 + 0 = 0$。

### 3. 计算样本空间 $\Omega$ 的总和与期望值

*   **计算 $\Omega$ 的总和 $S_{\Omega}$**：由于 $\Omega$ 是 $U$ 去掉点 $P(1, 1, 1)$ 后的集合，因此总和为：
    $$S_{\Omega} = S_U - X(P)$$
    点 $P(1, 1, 1)$ 对应的 $X$ 值为 $1 + 1 + 1 = 3$。
    $$S_{\Omega} = 0 - 3 = -3$$
*   **计算数学期望 $E[X]$**：
    $$E[X] = \frac{S_{\Omega}}{|\Omega|} = \frac{-3}{63} = -\frac{1}{21}$$

### 4. 结论

随机变量 $X$ 的数学期望值为 $-\frac{1}{21}$，对应选项 A。

\boxed{A}
Reasoning
Here's a thinking process to solve the problem:

1.  **Understand the Goal:** The objective is to find the expected value of the random variable $X$ defined on a specific sample space $\Omega$.

2.  **Analyze the Definitions:**
    *   **Set $U$:** The set of points $(x_1, x_2, x_3)$ where each coordinate $x_i$ is chosen from the set $\{-2, -1, 1, 2\}$.
    *   **Size of $U$:** Since there are 3 coordinates and 4 choices for each, $|U| = 4 \times 4 \times 4 = 64$.
    *   **Point $P$:** $P = (1, 1, 1)$.
    *   **Sample Space $\Omega$:** The notation $C_U P$ usually denotes the complement of the set $\{P\}$ in $U$. So, $\Omega = U \setminus \{P\}$. This means $\Omega$ contains all points in $U$ except $(1, 1, 1)$.
    *   **Size of $\Omega$:** $|\Omega| = |U| - 1 = 64 - 1 = 63$.
    *   **Random Variable $X$:** For a point $A(x_1, x_2, x_3) \in \Omega$, $X(A) = x_1 + x_2 + x_3$.
    *   **Probability Measure:** The problem says "randomly pick a point from $\Omega$". This implies a uniform distribution. Each point in $\Omega$ has probability $1/63$.

3.  **Formulate the Expected Value:**
    The expected value $E[X]$ is given by:
    $$E[X] = \sum_{A \in \Omega} X(A) \cdot P(A) = \frac{1}{|\Omega|} \sum_{A \in \Omega} X(A)$$
    Since $|\Omega| = 63$, we need to calculate the sum of $X(A)$ for all $A \in \Omega$.

4.  **Calculate the Sum of $X(A)$ over the full set $U$:**
    Let $S_U = \sum_{(x_1, x_2, x_3) \in U} (x_1 + x_2 + x_3)$.
    The sum can be split by linearity:
    $$S_U = \sum_{(x_1, x_2, x_3) \in U} x_1 + \sum_{(x_1, x_2, x_3) \in U} x_2 + \sum_{(x_1, x_2, x_3) \in U} x_3$$
    Due to symmetry (the set of values for each coordinate is the same), these three sums are equal. Let's calculate the sum of $x_1$ over all points in $U$.
    For a fixed $x_1$, how many points have this $x_1$? The other two coordinates $x_2, x_3$ can be any of the 4 values. So there are $4 \times 4 = 16$ points for each specific $x_1$.
    The possible values for $x_1$ are $\{-2, -1, 1, 2\}$.
    Sum of values for one coordinate: $(-2) + (-1) + 1 + 2 = 0$.
    So, the sum of $x_1$ over all 64 points is:
    $$16 \times (-2) + 16 \times (-1) + 16 \times 1 + 16 \times 2 = 16 \times (-2 -1 + 1 + 2) = 16 \times 0 = 0.$$
    Similarly, the sum of $x_2$ over all points is 0, and the sum of $x_3$ over all points is 0.
    Therefore, the total sum over the full set $U$ is:
    $$S_U = 0 + 0 + 0 = 0.$$

    *Alternative reasoning for $S_U$:*
    For every point $(x_1, x_2, x_3) \in U$, is there a symmetric point?
    If we map $(x_1, x_2, x_3) \to (-x_1, -x_2, -x_3)$, does it stay in $U$?
    The values are $\{-2, -1, 1, 2\}$. If $x \in \{-2, -1, 1, 2\}$, then $-x \in \{-2, -1, 1, 2\}$.
    Yes.
    So for every point $A \in U$, there is a point $-A \in U$ (where $-A = (-x_1, -x_2, -x_3)$).
    $X(A) = x_1 + x_2 + x_3$.
    $X(-A) = -x_1 - x_2 - x_3 = -X(A)$.
    Since the set $U$ is closed under negation and $P(1,1,1)$ maps to $(-1,-1,-1)$ which is also in $U$, the sum over all points in $U$ is 0.

5.  **Calculate the Sum of $X(A)$ over $\Omega$:**
    $\Omega = U \setminus \{P\}$.
    $P = (1, 1, 1)$.
    The value of $X$ at point $P$ is $X(P) = 1 + 1 + 1 = 3$.
    The sum over $\Omega$ is the sum over $U$ minus the value at $P$.
    $$S_{\Omega} = S_U - X(P)$$
    $$S_{\Omega} = 0 - 3 = -3$$

    Wait, let me double check the set $U$ definition.
    $U = \{(x_1, x_2, x_3) | x_i \in \{-2, -1, 1, 2\}\}$.
    Is $P(1,1,1)$ in $U$? Yes, $1 \in \{-2, -1, 1, 2\}$.
    Is $-P(-1,-1,-1)$ in $U$? Yes, $-1 \in \{-2, -1, 1, 2\}$.
    So the symmetry argument holds for the whole set $U$. The sum of coordinates over all points in $U$ is indeed 0.

    Let's verify the symmetry argument carefully.
    $U$ is the Cartesian product of $S \times S \times S$ where $S = \{-2, -1, 1, 2\}$.
    Sum of elements in $S$: $-2 + (-1) + 1 + 2 = 0$.
    Let $x$ be a random variable uniformly distributed on $S$. $E[x] = 0$.
    $X = x_1 + x_2 + x_3$ where $x_1, x_2, x_3$ are independent and uniformly distributed on $S$.
    $E[X]_{U} = E[x_1] + E[x_2] + E[x_3] = 0 + 0 + 0 = 0$.
    So the average value over $U$ is 0. The sum is 0.

    Now, we remove the point $P(1,1,1)$.
    $X(P) = 1 + 1 + 1 = 3$.
    The sum of $X$ values in $\Omega$ is $0 - 3 = -3$.

6.  **Calculate the Expected Value:**
    The number of points in $\Omega$ is 63.
    $$E[X] = \frac{S_{\Omega}}{|\Omega|} = \frac{-3}{63}$$
    Simplify the fraction:
    $$\frac{-3}{63} = -\frac{1}{21}$$

7.  **Review the Options:**
    A. $-\frac{1}{21}$
    B. $-\frac{1}{63}$
    C. $0$
    D. $\frac{1}{7}$

    My calculated result is $-\frac{1}{21}$, which matches Option A.

8.  **Double Check for Pitfalls:**
    *   **Set definition:** $x_i \in \{-2, -1, 1, 2\}$. Correct. No 0 involved.
    *   **Point P:** $(1, 1, 1)$. Correct.
    *   **Sample Space $\Omega$:** $C_U P$. Notation usually means Complement of P in U. "U minus P". Correct.
    *   **Summation:**
        Sum of $x_1$ over $U$: $4 \times 4 \times (-2 -1 + 1 + 2) = 0$.
        Sum of $x_2$ over $U$: $0$.
        Sum of $x_3$ over $U$: $0$.
        Total sum = 0.
    *   **Subtraction:** We removed $(1,1,1)$. Its contribution to the sum was $1+1+1=3$.
        New sum = $0 - 3 = -3$.
    *   **Count:** Total points = $4^3 = 64$. Removed 1 point. Remaining = 63.
    *   **Average:** $-3 / 63 = -1/21$.

    Let's check if there's any ambiguity in "C_U P".
    In set theory, $A^c$ is complement. Sometimes $C_S A$ is used.
    "Sample space $\Omega = C_U P$". This likely means $\Omega$ is the set of outcomes in $U$ excluding $P$.
    If it meant something else, like a combination or specific operation, it would be weird in this context. Given the context of probability on a set of points, "removing point P" is the standard interpretation.
    Also, the result $-1/21$ is exactly one of the options.
    If we didn't remove P, the expectation would be 0 (Option C).
    If we removed a point with sum 1, e.g., (1, -1, 1), sum would be -1, avg -1/63 (Option B).
    If we removed (-1, -1, -1), sum would be -(-3) = 3, avg 3/63 = 1/21. Not an option.
    If we added P? No, P is in U.
    What if $\Omega$ meant something else? "C" usually stands for Complement.
    What if $x_i$ could be 0? The set is explicitly $\{-2, -1, 1, 2\}$.
    What if P was not in U? $P(1,1,1)$ is in U.
    Everything seems consistent.

    Let's re-read the variable definition carefully.
    $X(A) = x_1 + x_2 + x_3$.
    $A = (x_1, x_2, x_3)$.
    Yes, straightforward sum of coordinates.

    Let's quickly check the calculation of sum over $U$ again.

Prompt
请解答以下数学题（多选题）。逐步推理，将最终答案放在 \boxed{} 中。

9. 设z = 3 + 2i，则（ ）
A. \bar{z} = 3 - 2i
B. |z| = 5
C. z^2 = 5 + 12i
D. \frac{z+3}{z-i} \in \mathbf{R}

请将答案放在 \boxed{} 中。
Response
根据题意，已知复数 $z = 3 + 2i$。我们逐项分析如下：

1.  **分析选项 A**：
    复数 $z = a + bi$ 的共轭复数定义为 $\bar{z} = a - bi$。
    对于 $z = 3 + 2i$，其共轭复数为 $\bar{z} = 3 - 2i$。
    故 **选项 A 正确**。

2.  **分析选项 B**：
    复数 $z$ 的模定义为 $|z| = \sqrt{a^2 + b^2}$。
    对于 $z = 3 + 2i$，模为 $|z| = \sqrt{3^2 + 2^2} = \sqrt{9 + 4} = \sqrt{13}$。
    选项中给出 $|z| = 5$，由于 $\sqrt{13} \neq 5$，故 **选项 B 错误**。

3.  **分析选项 C**：
    计算 $z^2$：
    $$z^2 = (3 + 2i)^2 = 3^2 + 2 \cdot 3 \cdot 2i + (2i)^2$$
    $$= 9 + 12i + 4i^2$$
    由于 $i^2 = -1$，代入得：
    $$= 9 + 12i - 4 = 5 + 12i$$
    故 **选项 C 正确**。

4.  **分析选项 D**：
    计算表达式 $\frac{z+3}{z-i}$ 的值：
    分子：$z + 3 = (3 + 2i) + 3 = 6 + 2i$
    分母：$z - i = (3 + 2i) - i = 3 + i$
    代入分式：
    $$\frac{z+3}{z-i} = \frac{6 + 2i}{3 + i} = \frac{2(3 + i)}{3 + i}$$
    由于 $3 + i \neq 0$，约分后得 $2$。
    因为 $2$ 是实数，即 $2 \in \mathbf{R}$，故该表达式属于实数集。
    故 **选项 D 正确**。

综上所述，正确的选项是 A、C、D。

Reasoning
The user wants me to solve a multiple-choice question involving complex numbers.

**Question Analysis:**
Given complex number $z = 3 + 2i$.
I need to check the validity of four statements:
A. The conjugate $\bar{z} = 3 - 2i$.
B. The modulus $|z| = 5$.
C. The square $z^2 = 5 + 12i$.
D. The expression $\frac{z+3}{z-i}$ belongs to the set of real numbers $\mathbf{R}$.

**Step-by-step Verification:**

**Step 1: Check Option A**
Definition of conjugate: If $z = a + bi$, then $\bar{z} = a - bi$.
Here, $a = 3$, $b = 2$.
So, $\bar{z} = 3 - 2i$.
Option A is correct.

**Step 2: Check Option B**
Definition of modulus: $|z| = \sqrt{a^2 + b^2}$.
Here, $|z| = \sqrt{3^2 + 2^2} = \sqrt{9 + 4} = \sqrt{13}$.
The option claims $|z| = 5$.
Since $\sqrt{13} \neq 5$ (because $5 = \sqrt{25}$), Option B is incorrect.

**Step 3: Check Option C**
Calculate $z^2$.
$z^2 = (3 + 2i)^2 = 3^2 + 2(3)(2i) + (2i)^2$
$= 9 + 12i + 4i^2$
Since $i^2 = -1$,
$= 9 + 12i - 4$
$= 5 + 12i$.
Option C claims $z^2 = 5 + 12i$.
Option C is correct.

**Step 4: Check Option D**
Calculate the value of $\frac{z+3}{z-i}$.
Substitute $z = 3 + 2i$.
Numerator: $z + 3 = (3 + 2i) + 3 = 6 + 2i$.
Denominator: $z - i = (3 + 2i) - i = 3 + i$.
Fraction: $\frac{6 + 2i}{3 + i}$.
To simplify, multiply numerator and denominator by the conjugate of the denominator ($3 - i$).
$\frac{6 + 2i}{3 + i} \cdot \frac{3 - i}{3 - i} = \frac{(6 + 2i)(3 - i)}{(3 + i)(3 - i)}$
Denominator: $3^2 + 1^2 = 9 + 1 = 10$.
Numerator: $6(3) - 6(i) + 2i(3) - 2i(i)$
$= 18 - 6i + 6i - 2(-1)$
$= 18 + 2$
$= 20$.
So, the fraction equals $\frac{20}{10} = 2$.
Since 2 is a real number, the expression belongs to $\mathbf{R}$.
Option D is correct.

**Conclusion:**
Options A, C, and D are correct. Option B is incorrect.
The question asks for a "multiple-choice question" (多选题), implying there might be more than one correct answer.

Let's double check calculations.
A: $\bar{3+2i} = 3-2i$. Correct.
B: $|3+2i| = \sqrt{9+4} = \sqrt{13}$. Option says 5. Incorrect.
C: $(3+2i)^2 = 9 + 12i - 4 = 5+12i$. Correct.
D: $\frac{3+2i+3}{3+2i-i} = \frac{6+2i}{3+i} = \frac{2(3+i)}{3+i} = 2$. Since $2 \in \mathbb{R}$. Correct.

$\frac{z+3}{z-i} = \frac{3+2i+3}{3+2i-i} = \frac{6+2i}{3+i} = \frac{2(3+i)}{3+i}$.
Since $3+i \neq 0$, we can cancel terms.
The result is 2.
2 is a real number. So D is correct.

So the correct options are A, C, D.

One minor thing: Could D be interpreted differently? $\frac{z+3}{z-i} \in \mathbf{R}$ means the result is real.
$z = x+yi$.
$\frac{x+yi+3}{x+yi-i} = \frac{(x+3)+yi}{x+(y-1)i}$.
For this to be real, the argument must be 0 or $\pi$, or simply the imaginary part is 0.
$\frac{(x+3)+yi}{x+(y-1)i} = \frac{((x+3)+yi)(x-(y-1)i)}{x^2+(y-1)^2}$.
Imaginary part of numerator: $y(x) + (x+3)(-(y-1)) = xy - (xy - x + 3y - 3) = xy - xy + x - 3y + 3 = x - 3y + 3$.
Set to 0: $x - 3y + 3 = 0$.
Check our $z$: $x=3, y=2$.
$3 - 3(2) + 3 = 3 - 6 + 3 = 0$.
So yes, it is real.

Prompt
请解答以下数学题（多选题）。逐步推理，将最终答案放在 \boxed{} 中。

10. 在空间中，A、B为两个定点，动点C到直线AB的距离为2，动点D到直线AB的距离为1。若二面角C - AB - D为60°，则（ ）
A. \angle CAD \ge 60^\circ
B. CD \ge \sqrt{3}
C. 当AB \perp CD时，CD \perp平面ABD
D. 当AB \perp平面ACD时，AC \perp AD

请将答案放在 \boxed{} 中。
Response
根据题意，我们可以建立空间直角坐标系来分析。设直线 $AB$ 为 $z$ 轴，定点 $A$ 为原点 $(0,0,0)$。

**几何建模：**
1.  动点 $C$ 到直线 $AB$ 的距离为 2，且在空间中移动，设 $C(2, 0, z_C)$。
2.  动点 $D$ 到直线 $AB$ 的距离为 1，且二面角 $C - AB - D$ 为 $60^\circ$，设 $D(\frac{1}{2}, \frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}, z_D)$。
3.  向量 $\vec{AC} = (2, 0, z_C)$，向量 $\vec{AD} = (\frac{1}{2}, \frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}, z_D)$。

**逐项分析：**

*   **选项 A：$\angle CAD \ge 60^\circ$**
    计算 $\cos \angle CAD = \frac{\vec{AC} \cdot \vec{AD}}{|\vec{AC}| |\vec{AD}|} = \frac{1 + z_C z_D}{\sqrt{4 + z_C^2} \sqrt{1 + z_D^2}}$。
    当 $z_C = z_D = 1$ 时，$\cos \angle CAD = \frac{2}{\sqrt{5}\sqrt{2}} \approx 0.632 > 0.5$。由于余弦函数在 $(0, \pi)$ 上单调递减，这意味着此时 $\angle CAD < 60^\circ$。因此该选项错误。

*   **选项 B：$CD \ge \sqrt{3}$**
    计算距离平方：$CD^2 = (2 - \frac{1}{2})^2 + (0 - \frac{\sqrt{3}}{2})^2 + (z_C - z_D)^2 = 3 + (z_C - z_D)^2$。
    因为 $(z_C - z_D)^2 \ge 0$，所以 $CD^2 \ge 3$，即 $CD \ge \sqrt{3}$。该选项正确。

*   **选项 C：当 $AB \perp CD$ 时，$CD \perp$ 平面 $ABD$**
    若 $AB \perp CD$，则 $\vec{AB} \cdot \vec{CD} = 0$。由于 $\vec{AB}$ 沿 $z$ 轴，这意味着 $C$ 和 $D$ 的 $z$ 坐标相等（$z_C = z_D$）。
    此时 $\vec{CD} = (-\frac{3}{2}, \frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}, 0)$。平面 $ABD$ 的法向量可由 $\vec{AB} \times \vec{AD}$ 求得，结果为 $(-\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}, \frac{1}{2}, 0)$。
    观察到 $\vec{CD} = \sqrt{3} \times (-\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}, \frac{1}{2}, 0)$，即 $\vec{CD}$ 与平面 $ABD$ 的法向量平行。因此，直线 $CD$ 垂直于平面 $ABD$。该选项正确。

*   **选项 D：当 $AB \perp$ 平面 $ACD$ 时，$AC \perp AD$**
    若 $AB \perp$ 平面 $ACD$，则 $AB \perp AC$ 且 $AB \perp AD$。这意味着 $C, D$ 均位于过 $A$ 且垂直于 $AB$ 的平面内。
    此时，平面 $ABC$ 与平面 $ABD$ 的二面角即为 $\angle CAD$。根据题意，该角为 $60^\circ$。
    而 $AC \perp AD$ 意味着 $\angle CAD = 90^\circ$，与 $60^\circ$ 矛盾。因此该选项错误。

综上所述，正确的选项是 B 和 C。

2.  **Setup the Geometry:**
    *   Let line $AB$ be the $z$-axis (or $x$-axis, or just a line). Let's visualize it.
    *   Points $A$ and $B$ are fixed. Let's place them for simplicity. Let $A$ be the origin $(0,0,0)$? Wait, the distance is to *line* $AB$. The positions of $A$ and $B$ along the line don't affect the distance to the line, but they affect the positions of $C$ and $D$ relative to $A$.

    *   Let line $AB$ be the $y$-axis. Let $A$ be at the origin $(0,0,0)$. Then $B$ is somewhere on the $y$-axis, say $(0, b, 0)$. Actually, the relative position of $A$ on the line matters for calculating distances $AC, AD$.
    *   Let's refine: Let line $AB$ be the $z$-axis. Let $A$ be the origin $(0,0,0)$. Then $B$ is at $(0,0, z_B)$.
    *   Point $C$ is a moving point. Its distance to line $AB$ is 2. So, if $C = (x_C, y_C, z_C)$, the distance to the $z$-axis is $\sqrt{x_C^2 + y_C^2} = 2$. Let's set coordinates such that $C$ is in the $xz$-plane for a moment? No, $C$ moves.
    *   Point $D$ is a moving point. Its distance to line $AB$ is 1. So $\sqrt{x_D^2 + y_D^2} = 1$.
    *   **Dihedral Angle:** The dihedral angle $C - AB - D$ is $60^\circ$. This means the angle between the plane containing $C$ and line $AB$ (let's call it $\alpha$) and the plane containing $D$ and line $AB$ (let's call it $\beta$) is $60^\circ$.
    *   Geometrically, if we project $C$ and $D$ onto a plane perpendicular to $AB$, say the $xy$-plane (assuming $AB$ is $z$-axis), the angle between the vectors from the origin to these projections is $60^\circ$ or $300^\circ$ (usually dihedral angle is defined within $[0, 180^\circ]$).
    *   Let $P_C$ be the projection of $C$ onto line $AB$. $P_C$ is on the $z$-axis. The vector $\vec{CP_C}$ is perpendicular to $AB$. $|\vec{CP_C}| = 2$.
    *   Let $P_D$ be the projection of $D$ onto line $AB$. $P_D$ is on the $z$-axis. The vector $\vec{DP_D}$ is perpendicular to $AB$. $|\vec{DP_D}| = 1$.
    *   The angle between $\vec{CP_C}$ and $\vec{DP_D}$ is the dihedral angle, which is $60^\circ$ (or maybe $120^\circ$ depending on orientation, but distance doesn't care about sign). Let's assume the angle between the perpendicular vectors is $60^\circ$.

3.  **Coordinate Representation:**

    *   Let $D$ have cylindrical coordinates $(r', \theta', z')$. Since distance to $AB$ (z-axis) is 1, $r_D = 1$.
    *   The dihedral angle is the difference in angles $\theta$ and $\theta'$. Let $\theta_C = 0$ (without loss of generality, rotate the coordinate system). Then $\theta_D = 60^\circ$ or $-60^\circ$. Let's pick $60^\circ$.
    *   Coordinates:

        *   Wait, if $A$ is just *a* point on the line, the setup is invariant under translation along the line. So we can assume $A$ is at $(0,0,0)$ for calculations involving lengths $AC, AD, CD$.
        *   Wait, $B$ is just another point on the line. It defines the line direction.

4.  **Evaluate Statement A: $\angle CAD \ge 60^\circ$**
    *   We need to find the angle between vectors $\vec{AC}$ and $\vec{AD}$.
    *   $\vec{AC} = (2, 0, z_C)$.
    *   $\vec{AD} = (1/2, \sqrt{3}/2, z_D)$.
    *   $\cos(\angle CAD) = \frac{\vec{AC} \cdot \vec{AD}}{|\vec{AC}| |\vec{AD}|}$.

    *   $\cos(\angle CAD) = \frac{1 + z_C z_D}{\sqrt{4 + z_C^2} \sqrt{1 + z_D^2}}$.
    *   We want to check if $\angle CAD \ge 60^\circ$, which means $\cos(\angle CAD) \le \cos(60^\circ) = 1/2$.

5.  **Evaluate Statement B: $CD \ge \sqrt{3}$**
    *   Calculate distance $CD$.
    *   $C = (2, 0, z_C)$.
    *   $D = (1/2, \sqrt{3}/2, z_D)$.

    *   $CD^2 = 9/4 + 3/4 + (z_C - z_D)^2 = 12/4 + (z_C - z_D)^2 = 3 + (z_C - z_D)^2$.
    *   Since $(z_C - z_D)^2 \ge 0$, $CD^2 \ge 3$.
    *   $CD \ge \sqrt{3}$.
    *   This inequality holds regardless of $z_C, z_D$.
    *   Equality holds when $z_C = z_D$ (i.e., $C$ and $D$ have the same projection onto line $AB$).
    *   So Statement B seems **TRUE**.

    *   Wait, distance to line is fixed at 2 and 1. So they are never close to the line.
    *   They are far from the line, but can be far from $A$ along the line.
    *   If $z_C, z_D \to \infty$, angle $\to 0$.
    *   So A must be false.
    *   Unless... Is $\angle CAD$ the angle between vectors $AC$ and $AD$? Yes.
    *   Is it possible $A$ is not on the line?
    *   "A, B为两个定点" (A, B are two fixed points). "直线AB" (Line AB). This is standard terminology. A lies on line AB.
    *   Let's check if there is any misinterpretation of "Dihedral angle C - AB - D".
    *   It's the angle between plane $ABC$ (which is plane containing line $AB$ and point $C$) and plane $ABD$ (plane containing line $AB$ and point $D$).
    *   Wait, if $C$ is on the line, the plane is undefined? But distance is 2, so $C$ is not on the line.
    *   The angle is $60^\circ$.
    *   Okay, let's re-read carefully.
    *   Maybe $C$ and $D$ are restricted? No.
    *   Maybe $A$ is not the origin? No, coordinates are translation invariant for angles if $A$ is the vertex.
    *   Wait, if $A$ is the origin, my calculation $\cos \theta \approx 1$ for large $z$ holds.
    *   Why would the problem setter put A as an option?
    *   Maybe I am missing a condition?
    *   "动点C" (Moving point C). "动点D" (Moving point D).
    *   Maybe $C, D$ are on a specific surface? No.
    *   Let's check Statement C and D. Maybe they give a hint.

7.  **Evaluate Statement C: When $AB \perp CD$, then $CD \perp$ plane $ABD$**
    *   Condition: $AB \perp CD$.
    *   Vector $\vec{AB}$ is along z-axis, say $\mathbf{k} = (0,0,1)$.
    *   Vector $\vec{CD} = D - C = (1/2 - 2, \sqrt{3}/2 - 0, z_D - z_C) = (-3/2, \sqrt{3}/2, z_D - z_C)$.
    *   Dot product $\vec{AB} \cdot \vec{CD} = 0 \cdot (-3/2) + 0 \cdot (\sqrt{3}/2) + 1 \cdot (z_D - z_C) = z_D - z_C$.
    *   So $AB \perp CD$ implies $z_D - z_C = 0$, i.e., $z_C = z_D$.
    *   This means the projection of $C$ and $D$ onto line $AB$ coincide. Let this point be $P$.
    *   So $C$ and $D$ lie in a plane perpendicular to $AB$ at $P$.
    *   Plane $ABD$ contains line $AB$ and point $D$.
    *   Since $D$ is in the plane perpendicular to $AB$ at $P$ (which passes through $A$? No, $P$ is just a point on line).
    *   Wait, plane $ABD$ is the plane defined by line $AB$ and point $D$.
    *   Since $AB$ is the z-axis, and $D$ is at $(1/2, \sqrt{3}/2, z_C)$, the plane $ABD$ is the plane passing through z-axis and vector $\vec{AD}$.
    *   Actually, since $z_C = z_D$, the vector $\vec{CD}$ is horizontal (perpendicular to z-axis).
    *   $\vec{CD} = (-3/2, \sqrt{3}/2, 0)$.
    *   Is $\vec{CD}$ perpendicular to the plane $ABD$?
    *   The plane $ABD$ contains the z-axis (vector $\mathbf{k}$) and the vector $\vec{AD}$? Or just $A, B, D$.
    *   Actually, plane $ABD$ is the plane containing the z-axis and point $D$.
    *   The normal vector to plane $ABD$ is perpendicular to $\mathbf{k}$ and $\vec{AD}$.
    *   $\vec{AD} = (1/2, \sqrt{3}/2, z_C)$.
    *   Normal $\mathbf{n} = \mathbf{k} \times \vec{AD} = (0,0,1) \times (1/2, \sqrt{3}/2, z_C) = (-\sqrt{3}/2, 1/2, 0)$.
    *   Wait, direction of $D$ from axis is $(1/2, \sqrt{3}/2)$. Normal to this radial vector in xy-plane is $(-\sqrt{3}/2, 1/2)$ or $(\sqrt{3}/2, -1/2)$.
    *   Let's check dot product of $\vec{CD}$ with $\mathbf{n}$.
    *   $\vec{CD} = (-3/2, \sqrt{3}/2, 0)$.
    *   $\mathbf{n} = (-\sqrt{3}/2, 1/2, 0)$.
    *   $\vec{CD} \cdot \mathbf{n} = (-3/2)(-\sqrt{3}/2) + (\sqrt{3}/2)(1/2) = \frac{3\sqrt{3}}{4} + \frac{\sqrt{3}}{4} = \frac{4\sqrt{3}}{4} = \sqrt{3} \ne 0$.
    *   So $\vec{CD}$ is not perpendicular to the normal, so not parallel to normal, so not perpendicular to plane?
    *   Wait, for a line to be perpendicular to a plane, the direction vector of the line must be parallel to the normal vector of the plane.
    *   Here, $\vec{CD}$ is parallel to $\mathbf{n}$?
    *   $\vec{CD} = (-3/2, \sqrt{3}/2, 0) = \sqrt{3} (-\sqrt{3}/2, 1/2, 0) = \sqrt{3} \mathbf{n}$.
    *   Yes! $\vec{CD}$ is a scalar multiple of $\mathbf{n}$.
    *   So $\vec{CD}$ is perpendicular to the plane $ABD$.
    *   Wait, let's verify the plane $ABD$.
    *   Plane $ABD$ contains line $AB$ (z-axis) and point $D$.
    *   Since $z_C = z_D$ (from $AB \perp CD$), $D$ has same z-coordinate as $C$.
    *   Wait, plane $ABD$ depends on $D$.
    *   Is $CD$ perpendicular to plane $ABD$?
    *   We found $\vec{CD} \parallel \mathbf{n}_{ABD}$.
    *   So $CD \perp$ plane $ABD$?
    *   Let's visualize.
    *   Line $AB$ is vertical.
    *   $C$ is at distance 2, angle 0.
    *   $D$ is at distance 1, angle 60.
    *   If $z_C = z_D$, $C$ and $D$ are in a horizontal plane.
    *   The vector $CD$ connects them.
    *   Plane $ABD$ is a vertical plane passing through $A$ (origin), $B$ (on z-axis) and $D$.
    *   This plane cuts the horizontal plane along the line $AD'$ (where $D'$ is projection of $D$). Actually, the intersection of plane $ABD$ and horizontal plane is the line $OD$ (where $O$ is projection of $A$ onto horizontal plane? No, $A$ is origin, which is on the line).
    *   Wait, $A$ is origin. So $A$ is in the horizontal plane? No, $A$ is on the line $AB$. The line $AB$ is the z-axis.
    *   Wait, if $A$ is origin $(0,0,0)$, and $C, D$ have $z=0$, then $C, D, A$ are all in $z=0$ plane.
    *   Then plane $ABD$ contains $A(0,0,0)$, $B(0,0,b)$, $D(1/2, \sqrt{3}/2, 0)$.
    *   Vectors in plane $ABD$: $\vec{AB} = (0,0,b)$ and $\vec{AD} = (1/2, \sqrt{3}/2, 0)$.
    *   Normal to plane $ABD$ is $\vec{AB} \times \vec{AD}$.
    *   $(0,0,1) \times (1/2, \sqrt{3}/2, 0) = (-\sqrt{3}/2, 1/2, 0)$.
    *   Vector $\vec{CD} = D - C = (1/2, \sqrt{3}/2, 0) - (2, 0, 0) = (-3/2, \sqrt{3}/2, 0)$.
    *   Is $\vec{CD}$ parallel to normal?
    *   $\vec{CD} = \sqrt{3} (-\sqrt{3}/2, 1/2, 0)$.
    *   Yes.
    *   So $CD \perp$ plane $ABD$.
    *   Wait, what if $z_C \ne 0$?
    *   If $z_C = z_D = k \ne 0$.
    *   $C = (2, 0, k)$, $D = (1/2, \sqrt{3}/2, k)$.
    *   $A = (0,0,0)$.
    *   $\vec{CD} = (-3/2, \sqrt{3}/2, 0)$.
    *   Plane $ABD$ passes through $A(0,0,0)$, $B(0,0,b)$, $D(1/2, \sqrt{3}/2, k)$.
    *   Vectors: $\vec{AB} = (0,0,b)$ (vertical), $\vec{AD} = (1/2, \sqrt{3}/2, k)$.
    *   Normal $\mathbf{n} = \vec{AB} \times \vec{AD}$.
    *   $(0,0,1) \times (1/2, \sqrt{3}/2, k) = (-\sqrt{3}/2, 1/2, 0)$.
    *   Wait, the z-component of normal is 0?
    *   Cross product of $(0,0,1)$ and $(x,y,z)$ is $(-y, x, 0)$.
    *   Yes. The normal to a plane containing the z-axis is horizontal.
    *   So normal is $(-\sqrt{3}/2, 1/2, 0)$.
    *   $\vec{CD}$ is $(-3/2, \sqrt{3}/2, 0)$.
    *   They are parallel.
    *   So $CD \perp$ plane $ABD$ holds.
    *   So Statement C seems **TRUE**.
    *   Wait, the condition was "When $AB \perp CD$".
    *   $AB \perp CD$ means $\vec{CD} \cdot \vec{AB} = 0$.
    *   $\vec{CD} = (-3/2, \sqrt{3}/2, z_D - z_C)$. $\vec{AB} = (0,0,1)$.
    *   Dot product is $z_D - z_C$.
    *   So condition $AB \perp CD$ implies $z_D = z_C$.
    *   And we proved that if $z_D = z_C$, then $CD \perp$ plane $ABD$.
    *   So C is correct.

8.  **Evaluate Statement D: When $AB \perp$ plane $ACD$, then $AC \perp AD$**
    *   Condition: $AB \perp$ plane $ACD$.
    *   This means line $AB$ is normal to plane $ACD$.
    *   Since $AB$ is the z-axis, the plane $ACD$ must be a horizontal plane (perpendicular to z-axis).
    *   For a plane to be horizontal, all points in it must have the same z-coordinate.
    *   Points in plane $ACD$: $A, C, D$.
    *   $A$ is at $(0,0,0)$ (assuming origin). So $z_A = 0$.
    *   Thus, for plane $ACD$ to be horizontal, we must have $z_C = 0$ and $z_D = 0$.
    *   Wait, is $A$ at origin? Yes, in my setup.
    *   Is it possible to shift $A$? No, $A$ is fixed. If $A$ is at $(0,0, z_0)$, then plane $ACD$ being perpendicular to $AB$ (z-axis) means $z_C = z_0$ and $z_D = z_0$.
    *   So $C$ and $D$ must have the same projection on line $AB$ as $A$ does?
    *   Actually, $C$'s projection is $P_C$, $D$'s projection is $P_D$.
    *   If plane $ACD \perp AB$, then $AB$ is perpendicular to every line in $ACD$ passing through intersection.
    *   Intersection of plane $ACD$ and line $AB$ is point $A$ (since $A$ is in plane and on line).
    *   So $AB \perp AC$ and $AB \perp AD$.
    *   $AB \perp AC \implies \vec{AB} \cdot \vec{AC} = 0$.
    *   $\vec{AB}$ is along z-axis. $\vec{AC}$ has z-component $z_C - z_A$.
    *   If $A$ is origin, $z_A = 0$. So $z_C = 0$.
    *   Similarly $z_D = 0$.
    *   So $C$ and $D$ must lie in the plane passing through $A$ and perpendicular to $AB$.
    *   In this case, $A, C, D$ form a triangle in a plane perpendicular to $AB$.
    *   Let's check the geometry in that plane.
    *   $A$ is origin.
    *   $C$ is at distance 2 from $A$ (since distance to line is 2 and $z_C=0$). So $C$ is on circle radius 2.
    *   $D$ is at distance 1 from $A$ (since distance to line is 1 and $z_D=0$). So $D$ is on circle radius 1.
    *   Angle $\angle CAD$ is the angle between vectors $\vec{AC}$ and $\vec{AD}$.
    *   Since dihedral angle is $60^\circ$, the angle between the planes is $60^\circ$.
    *   Wait, the dihedral angle is defined by the planes containing the line $AB$ and points $C, D$.
    *   If $C, D$ are in the plane perpendicular to $AB$ at $A$, then the plane containing $AB$ and $C$ is perpendicular to plane $ACD$?
    *   Let's clarify.
    *   Plane containing $AB$ and $C$ is defined by line $AB$ and point $C$.
    *   Plane containing $AB$ and $D$ is defined by line $AB$ and point $D$.
    *   Dihedral angle is angle between these two planes.
    *   If $C$ and $D$ are in the plane perpendicular to $AB$ at $A$, then the vectors $\vec{AC}$ and $\vec{AD}$ are perpendicular to $AB$.
    *   The angle between the planes is the angle between the lines $AC$ and $AD$ in the cross section plane perpendicular to $AB$.
    *   Wait, the angle between two planes intersecting at line $L$ is measured in a plane perpendicular to $L$.
    *   The plane $z=0$ (passing through $A$) is perpendicular to $AB$.
    *   The intersection of plane $ABC$ with $z=0$ is line $AC$.
    *   The intersection of plane $ABD$ with $z=0$ is line $AD$.
    *   So the dihedral angle is exactly $\angle CAD$.
    *   So if $AB \perp$ plane $ACD$, then $\angle CAD = 60^\circ$.
    *   The statement D claims: $AC \perp AD$.
    *   This means $\angle CAD = 90^\circ$.
    *   But we found $\angle CAD = 60^\circ$ (or $120^\circ$? No, dihedral angle is usually taken as acute or specified). Given as $60^\circ$.
    *   So $\angle CAD = 60^\circ$.
    *   Is $60^\circ = 90^\circ$? No.
    *   So Statement D is **FALSE**.

    *   Wait, let's double check.
    *   "二面角C - AB - D为60°".
    *   Usually dihedral angle is between $0$ and $180$.
    *   If planes intersect, angle is defined.
    *   If $AB \perp$ plane $ACD$, then plane $ACD$ is perpendicular to $AB$.
    *   This means $AB$ is normal to plane $ACD$.
    *   This implies $AB \perp AC$ and $AB \perp AD$.
    *   This implies $C$ and $D$ project onto $A$ on the line $AB$.
    *   So $C$ and $D$ are in the plane through $A$ perpendicular to $AB$.
    *   In this plane, the distance of $C$ from $AB$ is $AC = 2$.
    *   Distance of $D$ from $AB$ is $AD = 1$.
    *   The dihedral angle is the angle between plane $ABC$ (containing $AC$) and plane $ABD$ (containing $AD$).
    *   Since $AC \perp AB$ and $AD \perp AB$, the angle between the planes is the angle between $AC$ and $AD$.
    *   So $\angle CAD = 60^\circ$.
    *   Statement D says $AC \perp AD$, i.e., $\angle CAD = 90^\circ$.
    *   Contradiction.
    *   So D is false.

    *   Let's check if there is any ambiguity. "二面角" could be $120^\circ$? No, usually specified as 60. Even if 120, not 90.
    *   So D is definitely false.

9.  **Re-evaluating Statement A again.**
    *   Maybe I missed something.
    *   $A, B$ fixed. $C, D$ moving.
    *   Distance to line $AB$ is constant.
    *   Dihedral angle is constant.
    *   Is $\angle CAD$ constant? No.
    *   My previous counter-example: $C=(2,0,100), D=(0.5, \sqrt{3}/2, 100)$.
    *   $A=(0,0,0)$.
    *   $\vec{AC} = (2, 0, 100)$.
    *   $\vec{AD} = (0.5, \sqrt{3}/2, 100)$.
    *   $\cos \theta = \frac{1 + 10000}{\sqrt{4+10000}\sqrt{1+10000}} \approx \frac{10001}{100 \cdot 100} \approx 1$.
    *   Angle $\approx 0$.
    *   Is it possible $A$ is not origin?
    *   $A$ is a fixed point. Let's say $A$ is at $(0,0, h)$.
    *   Then $\vec{AC} = (2, 0, z_C - h)$.
    *   $\vec{AD} = (0.5, \sqrt{3}/2, z_D - h)$.
    *   If we choose $z_C$ and $z_D$ such that $z_C - h$ and $z_D - h$ are large and equal, angle is still small.
    *   So A is false.

    *   Wait, is it possible that "distance to line AB" means distance from point to line segment? No, "直线AB" means infinite line.
    *   Is it possible that $C$ and $D$ are constrained to be on the same side of $AB$? No.
    *   Is it possible $A$ is not on the line?
    *   "A, B为两个定点" - A and B are two fixed points.
    *   "直线AB" - The line passing through A and B.
    *   So A is on the line.
    *   Is it possible the problem meant "distance to segment AB"? Unlikely in this context.
    *   Is it possible that $C, D$ must form a triangle with $A, B$? No.
    *   Is there a constraint I missed?
    *   Maybe $\angle CAD$ is not angle at $A$? No, notation $\angle CAD$ is standard.
    *   Let's check if there's any geometric theorem I'm forgetting.
    *   Maybe the minimum angle is $60^\circ$?
    *   Let's minimize $\cos \theta$.
    *   $\cos \theta = \frac{1 + z_C z_D}{\sqrt{4 + z_C^2} \sqrt{1 + z_D^2}}$.
    *   We found for $z_C=z_D=k$, $\cos \theta \to 1$.
    *   We found for $z_C=0, z_D=0$, $\cos \theta = 1/2 \implies \theta = 60^\circ$.
    *   What if $z_C, z_D$ have opposite signs?
    *   Let $z_C = k, z_D = -k$.
    *   $\cos \theta = \frac{1 - k^2}{\sqrt{4 + k^2} \sqrt{1 + k^2}}$.
    *   If $k$ is large, $1-k^2 \approx -k^2$. Denominator $\approx k^2$.
    *   $\cos \theta \approx -1$.
    *   Angle $\approx 180^\circ$.
    *   So angle can be large too.
    *   But the claim is $\angle CAD \ge 60^\circ$.
    *   If angle can be close to 0, then A is false.
    *   Is there a constraint that $C, D$ are on the *same side* of the plane passing through $A$ perpendicular to $AB$? No.
    *   Wait, is $A$ an endpoint?
    *   Maybe "A, B are fixed points" implies $C, D$ are in a bounded region? No.
    *   Maybe "distance to line AB" means distance from $C$ to $A$ and $B$? No.
    *   Let's check the source or similar problems.
    *   Usually, these problems have specific configurations.
    *   Maybe $A$ is the projection? No.
    *   Let's re-read carefully: "A、B为两个定点" (A, B are two fixed points).
    *   Maybe the distance is from $C$ to $A$? No, "到直线AB的距离" (distance to line AB).
    *   Maybe $A$ is not on the line?
    *   If $A$ is not on the line, then $AB$ is a line passing through $B$ and some other point?
    *   "直线AB" means the line passing through points A and B. So A must be on the line.
    *   Unless "A, B" are just labels for two points, and "line AB" is defined by them. Yes.
    *   Wait, is it possible that $A$ is just a point in space, and the line is defined by two other points? No, "直线AB" explicitly uses A and B.
    *   Okay, assume my analysis is correct. A is false, B is true, C is true, D is false.
    *   Let's check B again.
    *   $CD^2 = 3 + (z_C - z_D)^2$.
    *   Minimum is 3 when $z_C = z_D$.
    *   So $CD \ge \sqrt{3}$.
    *   Is it possible to have $CD < \sqrt{3}$?
    *   $C$ distance 2, $D$ distance 1.
    *   Angle between their projections on plane perpendicular to $AB$ is $60^\circ$.
    *   In the cross-section plane, distance between projections $C'$ and $D'$ is $\sqrt{2^2 + 1^2 - 2(2)(1)\cos 60} = \sqrt{4+1-2} = \sqrt{3}$.
    *   Distance $CD = \sqrt{(C'D')^2 + (z_C - z_D)^2} = \sqrt{3 + (z_C - z_D)^2}$.
    *   So $CD \ge \sqrt{3}$ is always true.
    *   So B is definitely true.

    *   Let's check C again.
    *   "When $AB \perp CD$".
    *   This implies $z_C = z_D$.
    *   Then $CD$ is in the plane perpendicular to $AB$.
    *   Vector $\vec{CD}$ connects $(2,0,z)$ and $(0.5, \sqrt{3}/2, z)$.
    *   $\vec{CD} = (-1.5, \sqrt{3}/2, 0)$.
    *   Plane $ABD$ contains line $AB$ (z-axis) and point $D(0.5, \sqrt{3}/2, z)$.
    *   Normal to plane $ABD$ is perpendicular to $\mathbf{k}$ and $\vec{OD'} = (0.5, \sqrt{3}/2, 0)$.
    *   Normal is proportional to $(-\sqrt{3}/2, 0.5, 0)$.
    *   $\vec{CD}$ is proportional to $(-1.5, \sqrt{3}/2, 0) = \sqrt{3}(-\sqrt{3}/2, 0.5, 0)$.
    *   So $\vec{CD}$ is parallel to normal.
    *   So $CD \perp$ plane $ABD$.
    *   Wait, plane $ABD$ contains $A, B, D$.
    *   $A$ is origin $(0,0,0)$.
    *   $D$ is $(0.5, \sqrt{3}/2, z)$.
    *   If $z \ne 0$, plane $ABD$ passes through origin.
    *   Equation of plane $ABD$: Since it contains z-axis, equation is of form $ax + by = 0$.
    *   Passes through $D(0.5, \sqrt{3}/2, z) \implies 0.5a + \frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}b = 0 \implies a + \sqrt{3}b = 0$.
    *   Let $b=1, a=-\sqrt{3}$.
    *   Plane: $-\sqrt{3}x + y = 0$.
    *   Normal vector $\mathbf{n} = (-\sqrt{3}, 1, 0)$.
    *   Vector $\vec{CD} = D - C = (0.5 - 2, \frac{\sqrt{3}}{2} - 0, z - z) = (-1.5, \frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}, 0)$.
    *   Check if $\vec{CD} \parallel \mathbf{n}$.
    *   $\vec{CD} = \frac{\sqrt{3}}{2} (-\sqrt{3}, 1, 0)$?
    *   $\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2} (-\sqrt{3}) = -1.5$. Correct.
    *   $\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2} (1) = \frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}$. Correct.
    *   So yes, $\vec{CD}$ is parallel to normal.
    *   So $CD \perp$ plane $ABD$.
    *   So C is true.

    *   Let's check D again.
    *   "When $AB \perp$ plane $ACD$".
    *   This implies plane $ACD$ is perpendicular to $AB$.
    *   Since $A$ is on $AB$, plane $ACD$ must be the plane passing through $A$ perpendicular to $AB$.
    *   So $C$ and $D$ must be in this plane.
    *   This means $z_C = 0$ and $z_D = 0$ (if $A$ is origin).
    *   Then $C$ is at $(2,0,0)$ and $D$ is at $(0.5, \sqrt{3}/2, 0)$.
    *   $A$ is at $(0,0,0)$.
    *   Vectors $\vec{AC} = (2,0,0)$, $\vec{AD} = (0.5, \sqrt{3}/2, 0)$.
    *   Dot product $\vec{AC} \cdot \vec{AD} = 1$.
    *   $|\vec{AC}| = 2$, $|\vec{AD}| = 1$.
    *   $\cos \angle CAD = 1/2$.
    *   $\angle CAD = 60^\circ$.
    *   Is $AC \perp AD$? No, angle is 60, not 90.
    *   So D is false.

    *   So currently: B and C are True. A and D are False.
    *   The question is a "Multiple Choice Question" (多选题).
    *   Usually means one or more options are correct.
    *   Let's check if there's any subtlety in A.
    *   Maybe $A$ is not on the line?
    *   If $A$ is not on the line, say $A$ is at $(x_A, y_A, z_A)$ where $\sqrt{x_A^2+y_A^2} \ne 0$?
    *   But "直线AB" (Line AB) passes through A. So A must be on the line.
    *   Unless A and B are distinct points not collinear? No, two points define a line. A is on it.
    *   Maybe "A, B are two fixed points" just sets a reference frame?
    *   "直线AB" is the line passing through them.
    *   So A is on the line.
    *   Is there any constraint on $C, D$?
    *   Maybe $C, D$ are on the same side of the plane perpendicular to $AB$ at $A$?
    *   No, "moving points".
    *   Maybe the distance 2 and 1 are distances from $A$?
    *   "动点C到直线AB的距离为2" - Distance from point C to line AB.
    *   Standard interpretation is perpendicular distance.
    *   If it meant distance from A, it would say "AC=2".
    *   So my interpretation stands.

    *   Let's double check B.
    *   $CD \ge \sqrt{3}$.
    *   Is it possible for $CD$ to be smaller?
    *   Distance between two cylinders of radius 2 and 1 with angle 60?
    *   No, $C$ is on cylinder $r=2$, $D$ is on cylinder $r=1$.
    *   The angular separation is 60.
    *   Minimum distance occurs when they are at same height ($z$).
    *   Distance is chord length in circle? No, distance between points $(2,0)$ and $(1, 60^\circ)$ in polar.
    *   Cartesian: $(2,0)$ and $(1/2, \sqrt{3}/2)$.
    *   Distance squared: $(2-0.5)^2 + (0-\sqrt{3}/2)^2 = 2.25 + 0.75 = 3$.
    *   Distance $\sqrt{3}$.
    *   Any difference in $z$ increases distance.
    *   So $CD \ge \sqrt{3}$ is strictly true.
    *   So B is correct.

    *   Let's double check C.
    *   Condition: $AB \perp CD$.
    *   This means vector $CD$ is perpendicular to line $AB$.
    *   Since $AB$ is z-axis, $z$-component of $CD$ must be 0.
    *   So $z_C = z_D$.
    *   We verified that in this case $CD \perp$ plane $ABD$.
    *   Is there any edge case?
    *   What if $D=A$?
    *   Distance of $D$ to line $AB$ is 1. So $D$ cannot be on line $AB$. So $D \ne A$.
    *   What if $C$ or $D$ coincides with projection of $A$?
    *   $A$ is on line. Projections are on line.
    *   If $z_C = z_D$, $C, D$ are in a plane perpendicular to $AB$.
    *   $ABD$ plane contains $AB$ (vertical) and $D$.

    *   $AB \perp CD \implies z_C = z_D$.

    *   We need $\vec{CD} \cdot \vec{AB} = 0$ (True, $z$-diff is 0).
    *   We need $\vec{CD} \cdot \vec{AD} = 0$.
    *   $\vec{CD} = D - C = (x_D - x_C, y_D - y_C, 0)$.
    *   $\vec{AD} = (x_D, y_D, z_D)$.
    *   Dot product: $(x_D - x_C)x_D + (y_D - y_C)y_D + 0 \cdot z_D$.
    *   $= x_D^2 - x_C x_D + y_D^2 - y_C y_D$.
    *   $= (x_D^2 + y_D^2) - (x_C x_D + y_C y_D)$.
    *   We know $x_C^2 + y_C^2 = 2^2 = 4$.
    *   $x_D^2 + y_D^2 = 1^2 = 1$.
    *   So term is $1 - (x_C x_D + y_C y_D)$.
    *   $x_C x_D + y_C y_D$ is dot product of projections on xy plane.
    *   $\vec{p_C} \cdot \vec{p_D} = |\vec{p_C}| |\vec{p_D}| \cos(60^\circ) = 2 \cdot 1 \cdot (1/2) = 1$.
    *   So $1 - 1 = 0$.

    *   No. $P_1$ is a plane. $L$ is a line piercing it at point $A$.
    *   All lines in $P_1$ passing through $A$ are perpendicular to $L$?
    *   Yes, if $L \perp P_1$, then $L$ is orthogonal to every line in $P_1$ passing through the foot.
    *   So $AC \perp AB$ and $AD \perp AB$.
    *   So $AC$ and $AD$ are lines in the plane perpendicular to $AB$ at $A$.
    *   The angle between planes $ABC$ and $ABD$ is the angle between their traces in the plane perpendicular to $AB$.
    *   Trace of $ABC$ in plane $\perp AB$ at $A$ is line $AC$.
    *   Trace of $ABD$ in plane $\perp AB$ at $A$ is line $AD$.
    *   So angle between planes = angle between lines $AC$ and $AD$ = $\angle CAD$.
    *   Given angle is 60.
    *   So $\angle CAD = 60$.
    *   $AC \perp AD$ implies 90.
    *   So D is false.

    *   So $A < 60$.
    *   So A is false.

    *   So definitely B, C.
    *   Wait, if B and C are correct, and it's a multiple choice question, usually answers are listed like "BC".
    *   I will provide B, C.
